# L0 — LEDGER EVIDENCE

> Generated by `scripts/gauntlet/buildLedgerEvidence.py`. The ONLY admissible input
> to the `REBUILD_READINESS.md` rewrite: claims that were JUDGED and SURVIVED (§0).
> Gate **L0-B** independently re-checks every `[[claim-id]]` the rewrite ends up citing.

- **UPHELD** 1384 — survived, and where a critic ruled on the overturn, it holds.
- **NOT-UPHELD** 62 — TRUE, but the ledger sentence it names does not fall.
- **KILLED** 339 — refuted by its critics, or never judged at all (a LEAD, §0).

**556 surviving overturns land on 270 distinct ledger sentences.**
**61 claimed overturns were rejected** — the finding is
real, the deletion it asked for is not. Collapsing those two is how a readiness ledger
gets rewritten by findings that never actually contradicted it.

## Surviving overturns, grouped by the ledger sentence they name

### “**The stance tables (dmg 0.9/1.2/1.4, reach 300/200/100) are MASTERY-9 CEILING values, not base.**”

- **`[[cite-s27b/stance-damage-ceiling-is-mastery-10-not-mastery-9]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 contrasted)**)
  - RR:155-156's stance table is CORRECT but its label is off by one rung, and the web build gives the formula outright. §1t99s0§.as:3259-3271: Light = base * (0.5 + 0.4 * lightMastery * 0.1), Balanced = base * (1 + 0.2 * balancedMastery * 0.1), Heavy/Dual/Reversed = base * 1.4 flat. Solve them: mastery 0 gives Light 0.5 / Balanced 1.0 / Heavy 1.4 - exactly RR:156's level-1 Shugyosha row. Mastery 10 gives Light 0.9 / Balanced 1.2 / Heavy 1.4 - exactly RR:155's ceiling table. Mastery 9 gives 0.86 / 1.18, which matches NEITHER. The string table settles the cap independently: strings.json["8131"] = 'Light Stance Mastery Level 10.' (and 8132/8133 for Balanced/Heavy). So the ceiling is MASTERY-10, and the standing 'mastery-9' label in RR:155 and in the project memory is wrong by one. Android diverges and must not be ported: MCSPlayer.as:3205-3213 uses 0.5 + 0.5*m*0.01, 1 + 0.5*m*0.01 and a HEAVY of 1.5, on a 0-100 mastery scale.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3259-3271 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3202-3213 · docs/gauntlet/L0/index/strings.json["8131"] · docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:155-156`

Correct 'MASTERY-9' to 'MASTERY-10' in RR:155-156 and in the session memory. T

### “① coins-per-kill reality (`=8` is an **unconfirmed INFERRED seed**; 20+ samples show no XP formula either).”

*11 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/coins-per-kill-is-data]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - There is no coins-per-kill constant: coin drops are per-NPC <reward><loot> rows rolled independently, where loot id 0 pays 1 coin per qty and loot id 1 pays 5 coins per qty — the first bandit drops 0 or 2 coins at 50% chance, not 8.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:803-806 and :837-843 · data at reference/extracted/npcdata.xml (npc id 1)`
  - Lane impact: L-economy: blocker ① overturned in kind, not just in value — COINS_PER_KILL is the wrong shape of constant; early-game income is ~1 coin/kill.
- **`[[economy/calcxp-hard-cutoffs]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - calcXP carries two hard zero cut-offs and an elite doubler that the survey did not quote — the XP model is more completely held than claimed.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:166-190, with helpers at :94-110 (getGreyLevel, three bands) and :112-164 (getZeroDifference, eleven bands)`
  - Lane impact: L4/L10: the XP half of blocker ① is fully dead — grey-out floor, +3 over-level ceiling and elite ×2 included, portable today with no build needed. This also gives L4 (ENEMIES) its trivial-mob suppression rule for free.
- **`[[economy/coins-per-kill-and-xp]]`** [A2] (6 critics)
  - Both halves of blocker ① are HELD in the web build: coins per kill come from <reward><loot> id 0 (=1 coin × qty) and id 1 (=5 coins × qty) rolled against <chance>, or Math.random()×level for duelists — and calcXP is a complete, literal formula with both helper curves.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t68s0§.as:861-867 and :188-206 (= Android NPC.as:837-843 and :166-190); loot rows at reference/extracted/npcdata.xml:38-44 (131 <loot> blocks over 66 NPCs, tabulated this run)`
  - Lane impact: L4/L10: the commonest field mob yields 0.5 coins/kill in expectation (10% × 5 × 1c), not 8. src/config/economy.ts:37 KILL_REWARD={coinsFlat:8} and its docblock 'COINS have NO source formula found' are both refuted — the filmed +8 is the top of the duelist range floor(U[0,1)×9), so the frame stands a
- **`[[enemies-ai/kill-rewards-close-two-economy-blockers]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - Kill rewards are fully authored in npcdata's <reward> plus NPC.as: loot id 0 = a 1-coin drop, id 1 = a 5-coin drop, id 2 = quest item, id 3 = level-scaled random item, anything else = that literal item id — so coins-per-kill is Σ(qty × {1,5}) over rolled drops, not a flat 8; and the XP award is the complete grey-level / over-level / elite curve on base xp = level*2 + 20.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/npcdata.xml:38-44 (66 <reward> / 131 <loot>, chance domain 0.05,0.1,0.3,0.4,0.5,1) + reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:218, 799-855, 166-190, 94-105, 112-124`
  - Lane impact: Cross-lane into L0 economy, banked here because it lives in npcdata.xml. Kills economy blocker ① in both halves: the coin faucet and the full XP curve (incl. getGreyLevel playerLevel - floor(playerLevel/10) - 5, and getZeroDifference 5/6/…) are on disk.
- **`[[hunter-s26a/per-enemy-xp-formula-is-lvl-times-2-plus-20]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **android v0.682 (web parity not checked)**)
  - The per-kill XP formula is on disk and nobody claimed it: NPC.setLevel sets this.xp = lvl * 2 + 20, three lines below a quote npcdata/spawn-level-comes-from-the-quest-wave already made. Combined with NPCManager passing the quest-wave <lvl> into setLevel, every combat NPC's XP award is fully determined by its spawn level.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:239-259 (key line :252)`
  - Lane impact: L4 economy + L-progression. The 'no XP formula' half of :215 (1) is dead, and :200 (4) 'full XP-income model' gains its kill term. Note the clause survives on coins-per-kill, which this does not touch.

  - Lane impact: L4 economy: the 'no XP formula either' clause of RR:215 (1) dies at the TARGET build, not merely at v0.682. The coins-per-kill half of the same sentence still stands.
- **`[[itemdata/coins-per-kill-is-1-or-5]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - Coins per kill are not 8: npcdata's <reward><loot><id> is an enum, not an item id — 0 pays 1 coin per qty, 1 pays 5 coins per qty, 2 gives a quest item, 3 rolls a random item at the NPC's level; measured expectation over all 66 loot-bearing NPCs is min 0.50, max 10.00, mean 1.78 coins, and 429 of 495 NPCs drop nothing at all.

- **`[[lens3/coins-per-kill-half-of-RR215-is-also-dead-at-the-web-build]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (confirms android v0.682)**)

- **`[[nf4-the-per-kill-xp-formula-is-in-the-web-build-too]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717 (confirms android v0.682)**)
  - RR:215 blocker (1) ends '20+ samples show no XP formula either'. The web build has one. §1t68s0§.as:184-207 is the calcXP twin: it returns 0 when the mob is at or below a floor level (:186-189) or when 'param2 - param1 > 3' (:190-193), then scales base XP by '(1 + 0.05 * (mobLevel - playerLevel))' when the mob out-levels the player (:197) and by '(1 - (playerLevel - mobLevel) / §1t12069s0§())' when the player out-levels the mob (:201), doubling for elites at :203-206. It is called on death at §1t68s0§.as:888 with (StatsManager.getLevel(), this.level, this.xp, false). The android twin is NPC.as:166-189 calcXP. I record this separately because the coins finding asserts the XP half falls but sources that to another agent's finding rather than to an artifact; this is the artifact.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t68s0§.as:184-207, :888 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:166-189`
  - Lane impact: L4 economy / L4 progression: delete RR:215 blocker (1) whole. Per-kill XP is base <xp> from the NPC row, level-differenced, x2 for elite, zeroed beyond a 3-level gap.
- **`[[nf8-coins-per-kill-is-a-per-npc-loot-table-with-type-qty-and-chance]]`** [A2] (1 critic · build **web v0.717 for the roll, android v0.682 for the rows (C2)**)
  - The coins finding proves what the payout multipliers are (1x for loot type 0, 5x for type 1) but not where the type, quantity and probability come from, which is what 'coins-per-kill reality' actually asks. The roll is at §1t68s0§.as:820-828: it walks 'this.reward.loot', taking the type from §1t760s0§(), the count from getQty() and the probability from 'chance * 100', firing when '_loc6_ == 100 || Math.random() * 100 < _loc6_'. The rows themselves are in the NPC data — reference/extracted/npcdata.xml:39-42 is a real one, '<loot><id>0</id><qty>2</qty><chance>0.5</chance>', i.e. 2 coins on a coin-flip, and the file carries 131 <loot> blocks across its 495 npc rows. So per-kill coin income is fully tabulated, per enemy, not a constant awaiting measurement. Build caveat, stated plainly: the payout CODE is web v0.717, but npcdata.xml is android-sourced under C2, so the per-NPC rows are v0.682 until a web-side artifact says otherwise.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t68s0§.as:820-828, :861-867 · reference/extracted/npcdata.xml:39-42`
  - Lane impact: L4 economy: coins per kill = sum over the NPC's loot rows of (type 0 -> 1 * qty, type 1 -> 5 * qty), each gated by its own chance. There is no scalar to seed, so INFERRED `COINS_PER_KILL = 8` should be deleted, not re-labelled.

### “**Server-sim topology & save format** — nothing to copy; design a single-player persistence schema from the observed field set.”

*9 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/save-schema]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - The complete client-side save schema exists — 20 data slots plus 4 timer slots, each with a named serializer, a pipe-delimited wire format and a %54321 checksum — and the client ships its own offline SharedObject persistence path, so a single-player save model does not have to be invented.

### “① reach cap in **stage px** per stance (the one value the whole model needs; wiki gives "units" of unknown scale) — *measure blade-FX tip-to-body span in Light/Bal/Heavy from footage, or slash a dummy in a preserved buil”

*8 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/reach-formula]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - The blade-path reach cap is fully specified in stage pixels — baseSlashLength = 120 + floor((90 + 5*DAN) * SPEED / (20 + SPEED)), times a per-stance multiplier, with a hard 100 px floor on Heavy and Dual — and it is enforced on the drawn line at input time via InputManager.setMaxDistance.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1915-1919 (parity: reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2347); per-stance §1t99s0§.as:3094/3105/3116/3131/3142 with floors at :3119 and :3145; cap enforced §1t99s0§.as:3158 = android MCSPlayer.as:3094 'InputManager.setMaxDi`
  - Lane impact: L-combat-core — the highest-weight blocker in the system, closed without a measurement pass and without a playable build. Usable numbers at Dan 0: SPEED 0 → base 120 ⇒ Light 168 / Balanced 120 / Heavy 100(floor); asymptote → base 210 ⇒ Light 294 px max.
- **`[[combat-core/dan-scaled-slash-length]]`** [A2] (9 critics)
  - The base slash length is an exact formula in stage pixels that is scaled by Dan rank, so the combat-core 'reach in units of unknown scale' blocker is answered and its ceiling is owned by the rebirth system.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android_payload/scripts/StatsManager.as:1915-1919, desktop confirm reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2344-2348; BankUI.as:264`
  - Lane impact: combat-core blocker ① — units are stage pixels, floor 120, asymptote 210 px at Dan 0 rising to 255 px at Dan 9. Bank weight is 100 + DAN*10 + PAIDSTORAGE*10. Both are corroborated by the game's own prompt (string 610: 'Slash length and bank storage is increased with each dan rank').
- **`[[combat-core/reach-cap]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - Blocker ① is dead: reach is a source-exact stat formula, base = 120 + floor((90 + 5·danRank)·SPD/(20+SPD)), in 800×480 world units — not an unmeasured 'unit of unknown scale'.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1915-1919 (scale established by InputManager.as:341 and :229)`
  - Lane impact: L5 — the reach gate can be built today from a formula rather than waiting on a footage measurement; at the stated L1 character base = 124 world px.
- **`[[combat/web-stance-reach-multipliers-diverge-from-android]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (contrasted against android v0.682)**)
  - The WEB v0.717 build uses DIFFERENT per-stance reach multipliers from Android v0.682, so the reach model L1 must port is not the one the android decompile gives. Web: Light = base x1.4, Balanced = base x(1 + 0.2*balancedMastery*0.1), Heavy = base x(0.5 + 0.4*heavyMastery*0.1) with a hard floor of 100, Reversed = base x1.4, Dual = base x1.0 floor 100. Android: 1.5 / (1+0.5*m*0.01) / (0.5+0.5*m*0.01) / 1.5 / 1.0. At mastery 9 the balanced multiplier is 1.18 web against 1.045 android and heavy is 0.86 against 0.545. The SPD base term 120 + floor((90 + Dan*5)*spd/(20+spd)) IS identical in both builds (§1t1523s0§.as:2346-2347 vs StatsManager.as:1917-1918), and both feed InputManager.setMaxDistance, so only the multipliers diverge. Class identity is not guessed: §1t99s0§ is the MCSPlayer twin by its 15-arg initPlayer at :183-199 (param10 -> §1t4420s0§, declared = 10 exactly like baseSlashLength:int = 10 at MCSPlayer.as:107; param13/14 -> balanced/heavy mastery) and by :3158 calling §1t1541s0§.§1t10094s0§(this.§1t4417s0§), the setMaxDistance(slashLength) twin.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3094, :3105, :3116-3119, :3131, :3142-3145, :183-199, :3158; reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1541s0§.as:106-108; reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2344-2347`
  - Lane impact: L1 combat-core blocker ① dies — but on THESE constants. Any port of the android multipliers will miss the filmed build's reach on three of five stances. Also a general warning for this lane: combat tuning is exactly where v0.682 and v0.717 drift, so every combat number cited from shs3_android/ needs
- **`[[hunter-s27b/the-reach-cap-in-stage-px-the-one-value-the-whole-model-needs-is-in-the-web-payload]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 contrasted: Light x1.5, mastery on a 0-100 scale)**)
  - RR:190 blocker (1) calls the per-stance reach cap in stage px 'the one value the whole model needs' and prescribes measuring blade-FX span off footage. It does not need measuring: it is computed, in the web build. §1t99s0§.as:3094 sets §1t4417s0§ = §1t4420s0§ * 1.4 for Light, :3105 = §1t4420s0§ * (1 + 0.2 * §1t4422s0§ * 0.1) for Balanced, :3116-3119 = §1t4420s0§ * (0.5 + 0.4 * §1t4423s0§ * 0.1) for Heavy with a hard floor of 100. The android twin names the two fields: MCSPlayer.as:97 'slashLength', :107 'baseSlashLength', same three branches at :3030/:3041/:3052-3055. baseSlashLength comes from §1t1523s0§.as:2344-2348, 'var _loc2_:int = 90 + §1t7356s0§(§1t9426s0§) * 5; return int(120 + Math.floor(_loc2_ * param1 / (20 + param1)));' with param1 = SPD (x1.5 under the speed buff, :2336-2341) and §1t9426s0§ = STAT_DAN. And slashLength is spent as a LENGTH BUDGET on the drawn blade path, not a hitbox radius: android InputManager.as:51 'maxDistance:int = 200', :106-109 setMaxDistance, :198 'distanceLeft = maxDistance', :360 tapers the stroke as (maxDistance - distanceLeft)/maxDistance. So at dan 0 the base runs 120 px (SPD 0) asymptotically to 210 px, giving Light 168-294 px, Balanced 120-252 px, Heavy max(100, 60-189) px, and every rebirth raises the asymptote by 5 px.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3094, :3105, :3116-3119, :3158, :105 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2336-2348 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:97, :107, :182, :3030, :3041, :3052-3055, :3094 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/`
  - Lane impact: L5 combat-core blocker (1) closes as CONTRACT and the close-path instruction ('measure it from footage') should be struck. Also re-reads the ledger's 300/200/100 stance-reach row: those are Light-at-max, Balanced-at-base and the Heavy FLOOR of 100, not three ceilings.
- **`[[hunter-s27d/base-slash-length-is-a-closed-formula-combat-core-blocker-1-falls]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 supplies the names)**)
  - RR:190 combat-core blocker (1) calls the per-stance reach cap in stage px 'the one value the whole model needs' and prescribes measuring blade-FX tip-to-body span from footage. It does not need measuring. §1t1523s0§.as:2334-2348 computes it: §1t9592s0§() takes the SPD stat plus its bonus, multiplies by 1.5 under a speed buff, and returns §1t9593s0§(that), where §1t9593s0§(p) is 'var _loc2_:int = 90 + §1t7356s0§(§1t9426s0§) * 5; return int(120 + Math.floor(_loc2_ * param1 / (20 + param1)));'. The un-obfuscated Android twin names every term - StatsManager.as:1915-1919 'internal static function getBaseSlashLengthVal(speed:int) : int { var maxincrement:int = 90 + getPlayerStats(STAT_DAN) * 5; return int(120 + Math.floor(maxincrement * speed / (20 + speed))); }'. So BASE_SLASH_LENGTH = 120 + floor((90 + DAN*5) * SPD / (20 + SPD)), a saturating curve: SPD 0 -> 120, SPD 20 -> 165, SPD -> large -> 210 at DAN 0. Reach then = base x stance, per §1t99s0§.as:3094/:3105/:3116-3120 - Light x1.4 flat, Balanced x(1 + 0.02m), Heavy x(0.5 + 0.04m) floored at 100 absolute units. THIS ALSO SETTLES THE 300/200/100 TRIPLE: at a high-SPD, DAN-0 base of ~210 the MASTERY-0 row is 294 / 210 / 105, which is the ledger's 300/200/100. The triple is a mastery-ZERO row at high SPD, not a mastery-ceiling row - the ceiling multipliers are 1.4 / 1.2 / 0.9, which cannot make 3:2:1 off any single base. That independently confirms the arithmetic objection inside claim combat-core/stance-reach-is-asymmetric (whose OTHER objection, about RR:156's low endpoint, I refuted).
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2334-2348 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1905-1919 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3091-3146 · docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:155,:190`

Also strike '300/200/100 are MASTERY-9 CEILING values' from RR:155 - it is a mastery-0 row
- **`[[hunter-s27d/reach-cap-in-stage-px-per-stance-is-fully-recovered-the-biggest-combat-core-blocker-closes]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 diverges on the Light and Balanced multipliers)**)

BASE: §1t1523s0§.as:2334-2341 computes SPD_eff = STAT_SPEED + gear SPD, x1.5 while the speed buff is set, and passes it to :2344-2348, which returns 120 + floor((90 + DAN*5) * SPD_eff / (20 + SPD_eff)). That is a 120 px floor rising to a 210 px asymptote at dan 0 and 255 px at the dan-9 cap. PER STANCE: §1t99s0§.as:3091-3146 sets the live slash length as base x 1.4 (Light, :3094), base x (1 + 0.02 * balancedMastery) (:3105), base x (0.5 + 0.04 * heavyMastery) with a hard 100 px floor (:3116-3119), base x 1.4 (12224, :3131), and base x 1.0 with a 100 px floor (12223, :3142). BUFF_INJURED halves the base outright (:221-223, android MCSPlayer.as:220-222). The unit question is answered by the game's own UI: the stats panel prints int(baseSlashLength * slashModifier) as 'slash distance' — android InventoryUI.as:2232 in plain names, web §1t5957s0§.as:3013 with _loc21_ = §1t9593s0§(...) and _loc48_ the same three multipliers — so the number IS the drawn blade-path length. CROSS-CHECK against the ledger's own wiki row: multipliers 1.4 : 1.0 : 0.5 over a ~214 px base give 300 : 214 : 107, which is the wiki's '300/200/100' to rounding, and RR:156's independent 'Heavy's reach also scales x0.5 -> x0.9' is exactly 0.5 + 0.04m at m = 10. BUILD DIVERGENCE: android MCSPlayer.as:3030/3041/3052 uses 1.5, 1 + 0.005M, 0.5 + 0.005M — a v0.717 recreation must take the web numbers.

RR:88's KNOWN-UNKNOWN 'reach cap in px per stance' leaves that list, and the 'SPD **range**' half of RR:200 progression blocker ① closes with it. Config: BASE_SLAS

Critically, the coordinate space is not the raw mouse canvas: §1t1541s0§.as:343-344 converts the pointer to stage space ('_loc1_ = (mouseX - §1t9359s0§) / §1t9358s0§', same for y) and :349 rejects anything outside '§1t1523s0§.stageWidth' / 'stageHeight' before a single unit of budget is spent at :361-367. GameEngine.as:584 sets the tip string to 'Tip: In battle, you can use spacebar to change stance.' The matching web v0.717 input handler is §1t1541s0§.as:605-611: Keyboard.SPACE calls §1t4470s0§() and, if it passes, §1t4472s0§() to cycle the stance plus Snd_smallClick, else Snd_error. The gate §1t4470s0§ (§1t99s0§.as:3048-3051) is `§1t4469s0§() && this.§1t4414s0§ >= 48` — an action-state check AND the reload meter, no battle-state flag anywhere.

### “② War-Room **map topology** (board/adjacency)”

*7 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[lens3/web-parity-for-the-6x6-war-board-defaults-not-literals]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (confirms android v0.682)**)
  - The war board's android-only build label can be discharged, and the web build gets there by a different mechanism nobody has recorded. Android WarHexUI.as hardcodes 'this.ht = 6;' / 'this.wt = 6;' at :175-176 (the only two assignments in the file). The web v0.717 twin §1t7785s0§.as — same class by every structural marker: msgBoard, leftRoad/rightRoad, hexarrow, the mapid >> 12 sector math at :196-198, the §1t4646s0§ survivalmodedata embed — does NOT hardcode them: :162 declares 'param4:int = 6, param5:int = 6' and :176-177 assigns those parameters to ht/wt. The values survive anyway, because grep finds exactly one call site in the whole payload, §1t6483s0§.as:588, and it passes three arguments and takes both defaults. Same 55 px pitch and same (90, 92) origin on both sides (§1t7785s0§.as:208-209 vs WarHexUI.as:206-207). So the battlefield board is 6x6 in the TARGET build too — by default parameter rather than by literal, which is a distinction a citation audit will otherwise trip over.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t7785s0§.as:162, :176-177, :196-198, :208-209 + reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t6483s0§.as:588 + reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/WarHexUI.as:175-176, :206-207`
  - Lane impact: L-meta-social: the battlefield board is specifiable at the TARGET build — 6x6, 55 px pitch, origin (90, 92) — with no C2 hedge left. Only the per-hex state feed is server-side.

- **`[[meta-social/world-map-topology-is-a-bitmap-mask]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **android v0.682 blob, but the coordinate transform is confirmed against a web v0.717 frame**)
  - The world-map / War-Room board topology is HELD and it is a bitmap, not a mystery. reference/extracted/android/110_Mobile_MapBitmapData.bin is a 63x72 two-colour GIF with 735 white pixels on black — a pixel silhouette of Japan (Hokkaido, Honshu, Shikoku, Kyushu and the Ryukyu chain all legible at x8 nearest-neighbour). White = a territory exists. WorldMapUI.as:206-208 hasTerritory() is mapBitmapData.getPixel(territoryx + TERRSHIFTX - WARMARKERSHIFTX, territoryy + TERRSHIFTY) != 0, with WarSelectUI.as:15/17/19 giving WARMARKERSHIFTX=8, TERRSHIFTX=21, TERRSHIFTY=51; getHexOwner keys ownership by mapid = (x+341) << 12 | (y+341); QuestEngine.as:1206 uses the same mask. CROSS-VALIDATED AGAINST A FRAME: the displayed 'NAGANO (26,52)' in frame_0025.jpg maps to getPixel(26-8, 52) = getPixel(18,52), which I read as pure white — land. So the board is 735 territories on a W x H grid with holes, and adjacency is grid-neighbour.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/android/110_Mobile_MapBitmapData.bin (63x72 GIF, 735 non-black pixels, my own PIL read incl. pixel(18,52) = (255,255,255)); reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/WorldMapUI.as:206-208, :371-390 and WarSelectUI.as:15, :17, :19; frame reference/frames/zFtCaTO0_1M/map/frames/fra`
  - Lane impact: L0 meta-social + L10 world-exploration. Kills :245 ② outright, and also falsifies :115's unknown-unknown ('sparse geographic Japan outline, not a grid you can generate from W×H' — it IS a W×H grid plus a mask) and makes :225 ①'s close-path ('trace the map image') redundant, since the image is alread
- **`[[nf6-war-board-adjacency-is-in-code-not-just-the-board-dimensions]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **android v0.682 (the web twin §1t7785s0§.as is the same class by every structural marker but the getExitOwner body was not separately line-cited web-side — C2 hedge stands on this one)**)
  - RR:245 blocker (2) is 'War-Room map topology (board/adjacency)' — two halves — and the 6x6 finding argues only the board half, so the adjacency half would otherwise survive the rewrite. It does not. WarHexUI.as:443-463 getExitOwner IS the adjacency rule: it derives the territory's own war-map coordinates as 'territoryx = (this.mapid >> 12 & 0x0FFF) - 341' and 'territoryy = (this.mapid & 0x0FFF) - 341', then resolves each edge column and row to the neighbouring territory — xpos 0 to (territoryx - 1, territoryy), xpos 5 to (territoryx + 1, territoryy), ypos 0 to (territoryx, territoryy - 1), ypos 5 to (territoryx, territoryy + 1) — and returns 0 for interior cells. That is four-neighbour orthogonal territory adjacency with an explicit 341 coordinate origin, and it is what drives the frontline logic at :213-218 and :259-307. Both halves of RR:245 (2) fall together.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/WarHexUI.as:443-463, :213-218, :259`
  - Lane impact: L-meta-social: delete RR:245 blocker (2) rather than half of it. What remains open is only the live per-hex ownership feed, already covered by RR:245 blocker (1).
- **`[[s26d/war-room-battlefield-board-is-a-hardcoded-6x6-grid]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **android v0.682**)
  - The War-Room battlefield board's dimensions are NOT server-supplied and ARE recoverable from the shipped code. WarHexUI.as:175-176 set 'this.ht = 6;' and 'this.wt = 6;' as plain literals, and grep over the whole file shows these are the ONLY two assignments to wt or ht anywhere in it. The hex layout is hardcoded at the same pitch: :253 'hex.x = 90 + xpos * 55;' :254 'hex.y = 92 + ypos * 55;', with the terrain layer using the identical transform at :206-207. The pipe-delimited server string parsed at :231-252 carries per-hex STATE only - a 7-field record of xpos, ypos, owner, side and strength. This overturns hunter-s26a/war-room-battlefield-board-is-server-dimensioned and partially closes REBUILD_READINESS ② 'War-Room map topology (board/adjacency)': the board is a 6x6 grid at 55 px pitch with origin (90, 92), and its adjacency follows from that.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/WarHexUI.as:175-176, :206-207, :231-232, :239, :253-254`
  - Lane impact: L-meta-social: the battlefield board can be specified today - 6x6, 55 px pitch, origin (90, 92). Only the per-hex state feed is server-side.

### “3. **The stance tables (dmg 0.9/1.2/1.4, reach 300/200/100) are MASTERY-9 CEILING values, not base.**”

*7 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-5/stance-mastery-caps-at-10-not-9]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - Stance mastery caps at level 10, not 9. The three stance-mastery skills each enumerate levels 1..10, which is why the web damage curve only reaches Light x0.9 / Balanced x1.2 / Heavy x1.4 at mastery 10. Every 'mastery-9 ceiling' label in the project docs is off by one.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/skilldata.xml (skills 1000, 1001, 1002) + reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3262, :3265, :3270 + docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:155`
  - Lane impact: L5 combat-core — if the build 'targets mastery-9' as REBUILD_READINESS.md:157 offers, the correct ceiling is 0.86/1.18/1.4, not 0.9/1.2/1.4; the published table is the mastery-10 row.
- **`[[hunter-s26a/web-stance-ceilings-are-mastery-10-not-9]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - The web mastery coefficients hit the ledger's 0.9 / 1.2 / 1.4 stance table EXACTLY at mastery 10, not mastery 9. §1t99s0§.as:3262 gives LIGHT = base * (0.5 + 0.4*lightMastery*0.1) and :3265 BALANCED = base * (1 + 0.2*balancedMastery*0.1); at mastery 10 those are 0.90 and 1.20 exactly, with HEAVY fixed at 1.4 (:3270). At mastery 9 they are 0.86 and 1.18. REBUILD_READINESS.md:155 labels 0.9/1.2/1.4 'MASTERY-9 CEILING values'; for the web build the exact-fit ceiling is mastery 10. The same *0.1 scale appears in the reach switch (:3105, :3116), so the correction propagates to reach as well.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3262, :3265, :3270, :3105, :3116 + docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:155`
  - Lane impact: L-combat-core. PARTIAL, not HELD: the arithmetic is certain but I did not locate the mastery CAP, so 'mastery-9' may still be right about the reachable maximum while being wrong about which mastery reproduces the table. The rewrite should state the coefficient, not the label.
- **`[[kh-web-getdamage-twin-crit-is-1.3]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **both (divergent)**)

WEB v0.717: maxreload = 8*24 = 192 frames (§1t99s0§.as:249) against android's 5*24 = 120 (MCSPlayer.as:239). Recharge is +1 per tick while reload < maxreload (web §1t99s0§.as:1203-1205; android MCSPlayer.as:1158-1160); the +6 is bonusReload(), whose only caller is NPCManager.as:1711 inside `if(deathCount > 0)` — a KILL bonus (web twin §1t99s0§.as:3320-3323). The stance gate is reload >= 48 in web (§1t99s0§.as:3050, :3060) vs reload < maxreload/6 = 20 in android (MCSPlayer.as:2977). Identical in both: zero-at-full on a slash, and the x0.5 / x1 / x1.5 per-stance drain. Nothing about this system needs measuring — but the android numbers are the wrong build's.

### “③ Focus numerics (max/fill/switch-cost).”

*6 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-B/web-focus-numerics-diverge-from-android-on-four-constants]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717 (contrasted with android v0.682)**)
  - The Focus/reload model differs between builds on four constants, and the web values are the ones a v0.717 target needs. maxreload is 8*24 = 192 in web (§1t99s0§.as:249) against 5*24 = 120 in android (MCSPlayer.as:239). The stance-switch cost is a FLAT -48 in web (§1t99s0§.as:3162-3165) against 'reload > max/2 -> max/3, else -= max/6' in android (MCSPlayer.as:3098-3105). The stance-switch gate is 'reload >= 48' in web (§1t99s0§.as:3050 and :3060) against 'reload < max/6' in android (MCSPlayer.as:2977). The HUD normalisation is (reload-48)/(max-48) in web (§1t113s0§.as:326-328) against (reload-24)/(max-24) in android (MCSPlayer.as:3272). Unchanged across builds: +1 fill per tick, the per-stance slash drain (x0.5 light / x1 balanced+reversed / x1.5 heavy+dual), zero-on-crit-slash, and the +6 kill bonus. This closes RR:195 (3) 'Focus numerics (max/fill/switch-cost)' and RR:190 (5) on WEB constants.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:249, :1203-1205, :3050, :3060, :3162-3165, :3295-3314, :3318-3325 + reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t113s0§.as:326-328 vs reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:239, :1158-1168, :2977, :3098-3105, :3240-3272`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters + L5 combat-core: the blocker closes, but only on the WEB constants. Porting the android Focus model to a v0.717 target is wrong on max, switch-cost, gate and HUD scale simultaneously.
- **`[[hunter/focus-plus6-is-a-kill-bonus]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **both**)
  - The +6 Focus reward fires on a KILL, not on a landed slash. Both builds gate bonusReload() behind a count of enemies that just died this resolution pass. This inverts the meter economy the packet describes: connecting gives nothing back, so a long single-target duel drains Focus monotonically until the enemy dies.

  - The web v0.717 Focus bar's hidden dead zone is 48, not the 24 the packet reports from the Android build. ### “The "271 cap" is gear/rebirth-inclusive (237-point first-run budget makes it impossible in one stat) — resolvable by arithmetic now.”

*5 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[hunter-s26c/the-271-cap-is-the-web-per-stat-cap-at-level-80]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 contrasted)**)
  - REBUILD_READINESS.md:201 says the "271 cap" is gear/rebirth-inclusive and that a 237-point first-run budget makes 271 impossible in one stat, then files it as 'resolvable by arithmetic now'. It is not an arithmetic puzzle and it is not gear-inclusive: 271 is the literal hard-coded per-stat ceiling of the WEB build at the level cap. §1t5957s0§.as:3340 reads the character level into _loc2_ and :3344-3346 gate the ATK / DEF / SPD add buttons on 'stat < 1 + (_loc2_ + 10) * 3'. At level 80 that is 1 + 90*3 = 271 EXACTLY. The same block gives the other three ceilings for free: MAXHP < 10 + (level+10)*10 = 910 at L80, MAXCHI < 10 + (level+10)*5 = 460, MAXAP < 30 + (level+10)*5 = 480. Android's numbers are different (5 / 5 / 1 / 1 coefficients, InventoryUI.as:2407-2412) and do NOT produce 271, which is a second confirmation that 271 is a v0.717 web datum. No reconstruction needed, and the 237-point-budget reasoning in the ledger is answering a question the code never asked.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t5957s0§.as:3340-3346 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/InventoryUI.as:2406-2412 · docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:201`
  - Lane impact: L4 progression: replace RR:201's surface note with four exact cap formulas, ATK/DEF/SPD 1+(lvl+10)*3, MAXHP 10+(lvl+10)*10, MAXCHI 10+(lvl+10)*5, MAXAP 30+(lvl+10)*5, and delete the gear/rebirth explanation of 271.
- **`[[lens3/271-is-reachable-in-one-stat-the-budget-is-237-and-the-cost-is-90]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - Settles a contradiction between two findings that both claim RR:201. s26c/the-271-cap-IS-the-shipped-level-80-base-stat-cap says 271 'is unreachable in one stat ... the panel stops granting'; UPHELD progression/271-cap-explained says it 'IS reachable in one stat on a first run'. The arithmetic is entirely inside one function and settles it for the second: §1t5957s0§.as:3457 computes the ATK points already spent as (ATK - 1) / 3, which pins base ATK = 1 and the step = 3; :3422 confirms the step ('§1t9713s0§(...§1t9408s0§,3)'); :3461 sets the entire first-run budget to (level - 1) * 3, i.e. 237 at the level cap of 80; :3344 caps ATK at 1 + (level + 10) * 3 = 271. Lifting ATK from 1 to 271 costs (271 - 1) / 3 = 90 of 237 points, and the gate is a strict '<', so the last grant fires at 268 and lands exactly on 271. 271 is reachable in ONE stat with 147 points to spare. The ledger's parenthetical ('a 237-point first-run budget makes it impossible in one stat') is dead, and so is the same claim where the overturning finding repeats it.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t5957s0§.as:3340-3346, :3422, :3454-3461`
  - Lane impact: L4 progression: RR:201's whole surface note goes. Ship STAT_CAP_{ATK,DEF,SPD} = 1 + (level+10)*3, base 1 step 3, budget (level-1)*3 — and the same block gives the anti-cheat spend audit (:3454-3465) that fires when spent points exceed the budget.
- **`[[nf7-271-cap-is-neither-gear-nor-rebirth-inclusive]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717 for the cap and accessor, android v0.682 for the budget grant (which the web build corroborates at §1t5957s0§.as:3461)**)
  - The 271-cap finding kills RR:201's parenthetical but leaves its headline — 'The "271 cap" is gear/rebirth-inclusive' — standing. Both adjectives are false. GEAR: the panel's cap gate and the anti-cheat audit both read §1t9714s0§ (§1t5957s0§.as:3344, :3457), and §1t9714s0§ is getStat, proven by the android reinforce twin where §1t401s0§.as:1112 '§1t9714s0§(20064)' lines up with InteractUI.as:1104 'StatsManager.getStat(20064)'. Gear bonuses ride a SEPARATE accessor, §1t7358s0§ / findBonusStat, which is added on at the point of use — §1t1523s0§.as:2336 '§1t7356s0§(§1t9410s0§) + §1t7358s0§(§1t9410s0§)' — and never enters the cap comparison. REBIRTH: StatsManager.as:2135 sets the stat budget to '(getLevel() - 1) * 3' with no STAT_DAN term, in pointed contrast to the skill budget twenty lines later at :2156, 'getLevel() - 1 + getPlayerStats(STAT_DAN) * (levelCap - 1)', which does carry one. Rebirth multiplies SKILL points, not STAT points. 271 is a plain base-stat ceiling: 1 + (level + 10) * 3.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t5957s0§.as:3344, :3457, :3461 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t401s0§.as:1112 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/InteractUI.as:1104 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:2125-2127, :2135, :2156 · reference/deco`
  - Lane impact: L4 progression: strike the whole RR:201 sentence, not just its parenthetical. Ship STAT_CAP = 1 + (level + 10) * 3 as a BASE-stat cap, STAT_BASE = 1, STAT_STEP = 3, STAT_BUDGET = (level - 1) * 3, gear applied above the cap at the point of use.
- **`[[progression/271-cap-explained]]`** [A2] (9 critics)
  - The "271 cap" is exactly the level-80 evaluation of the ATK/DEF/SPD cap formula 1+(80+10)*3 = 271 — a hard per-stat UI ceiling, not a gear/rebirth-inclusive total, and it IS reachable in one stat on a first run.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t5957s0§.as:3344 (cap formula) + §1t1523s0§.as:362 (levelCap 80) + §1t1523s0§.as:2577 (237-point budget)`
  - Lane impact: L4: the Surface bullet's arithmetic is refuted; it assumed +1 per point. Replace with the cap formula.
- **`[[s26c/the-271-cap-IS-the-shipped-level-80-base-stat-cap]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - RR:201's explanation of the '271 cap' is wrong, and the web build says so in one line of arithmetic. §1t5957s0§.as:3344-3346 gate the ATK/DEF/SPD add-buttons on 'stat < 1 + (level + 10) * 3'. At the level cap of 80 that is 1 + 90 * 3 = 271 EXACTLY. So 271 is not a gear/rebirth-inclusive total that a 237-point first-run budget makes unreachable in one stat — it is the shipped per-stat hard ceiling for ATK, DEF and SPD, straight from the target build, and it is unreachable in one stat for a much simpler reason: the panel stops granting. The companion web ceilings from the same block are MAXHP 10 + (level + 10) * 10 = 910 at L80, MAXCHI 10 + (level + 10) * 5 = 460, MAXAP 30 + (level + 10) * 5 = 480. This is a strict strengthening of progression/per-stat-caps-are-a-level-formula, which noticed the formulas but never substituted the level cap and so never saw the 271 identity.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t5957s0§.as:3340-3346`
  - Lane impact: L4 progression — RR:201's surface note is not merely replaceable, it is factually wrong about what 271 is. Ship STAT_CAP_ATK/DEF/SPD = 1 + (level + 10) * 3 as CONTRACT and delete the gear/rebirth reconciliation.

### “⑤ severed-limb death variant.”

- **`[[NF-E/bindata-parity-map-21-of-27-identical-and-a-spider-only-in-web]]`** [A3] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 vs android v0.682**)

Six differ: ogre (100, 184,193 vs 184,120), skills (102, 333,768 vs 338,096), guardian (111, 117,638 vs 117,596), spear (113, 153,258 vs 132,270), survivalmodedata (119) — and 114_1t4641s0.bin, a SPIDER animation set of 10 anims (spider_idle, spider_walk, spider_attackstart, spider_attackend, spider_lunge, spider_death, spider_flinch, spider_descend, spider_dig, spider_death2) with NO android counterpart at all. An enemy type that exists only in the target build.

Note this is NOT the circular diff C2 forbids: bindata/ is extracted from the WEB payload, not from the android blobs.

### “① DEF mitigation curve + SPD range/cooldown scaling (wiki gives only direction) — *measure per-point deltas in a build.*”

*5 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-3-def-mitigation-is-flat-subtraction-both-builds]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **both**)
  - DEF mitigation is FLAT SUBTRACTION in both builds and no curve exists. Damage is reduced by `damage -= getDefense()`, and getDefense returns `defense * 0.8` only when the DEFENDER is in Heavy stance, otherwise `defense`. This closes the DEF half of REBUILD_READINESS.md:200 progression blocker ① ('DEF mitigation curve … measure per-point deltas in a build') — there is no build to measure and no curve to find. ### “④ SPD→cooldown formula.”

*5 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-4-spd-to-cooldown-formula-recovered]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **android v0.682 (web parity not separately checked; the sibling getBaseSlashLengthVal IS identical in both builds)**)
  - The SPEED-stat → skill-cooldown transfer function is a shipped two-line formula: cooldownModifier = 1 - min(speed, 300) / 300 * 0.5, i.e. a linear halving that saturates at SPD 300. It is consumed by the character sheet at InventoryUI.as:1937 and printed to the player at :2233 ('skill cooldown modifier: 0.' + int(mod*1000)). This closes skills-meters blocker ④ at REBUILD_READINESS.md:195 and the SPD half of progression blocker ① at :200.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1854-1860, consumed at InventoryUI.as:1937 and printed at :2233`

- **`[[NF-C/web-spd-to-cooldown-saturates-at-600-not-300]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717 (contrasted with android v0.682)**)
  - The SPEED-stat → skill-cooldown transfer function exists in the WEB build too, and its saturation point is 600, not the android build's 300: cooldownModifier = 1 - min(speed, 600) / 600 * 0.5. It is reached from §1t1523s0§.as:2279-2286 (STAT_SPEED = §1t9410s0§ = 8, x1.5 while the speed buff is up) and consumed at §1t105s0§.as:318, where a skill's cooldown is levelList[level].cooldown * 24 multiplied by the modifier. Porting the android constant halves the benefit at any given SPD (at SPD 300 android is already at the 0.5 floor while web is at 0.75).
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2289-2296 and :2279-2286 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t105s0§.as:297-319 vs reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1854-1860`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters ④ closes at the TARGET build — and closes with 600, not the 300 an android-sourced claim would write into a v0.717 config.
- **`[[NF-D/web-spd-to-cooldown-saturates-at-600-not-300]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717 (contrasted with android v0.682)**)
  - The web v0.717 SPEED-to-skill-cooldown transfer function saturates at SPD 600, not SPD 300: cooldownModifier = 1 - min(speed, 600) / 600 * 0.5. The shape is the same linear halving as android's, but the knee is at twice the speed, so at any given SPD below 300 the web build gives HALF the cooldown reduction the android formula does (e.g. SPD 150 -> 0.875 web vs 0.75 android). The wrapper is the structural twin of android getSkillCooldownModifier: it reads stat §1t9410s0§ = 8 (= STAT_SPEED, android StatsManager.as:186) and applies the same *= 1.5 buff branch before delegating.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2279-2296, :220 vs reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1845-1861, :186`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters: the blocker closes, but the ledger must carry the WEB constant 600. NF-4-spd-to-cooldown-formula-recovered supplies the correct android formula and honestly flagged web parity as unchecked - this discharges that flag, and it diverges.
- **`[[hunter/web-speed-cooldown-cap-is-600]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717**)

- **`[[skills-meters/spd-cooldown-cap-600]]`** [A2] (6 critics)

  - Lane impact: L6 art-anim-audio-feel: the 18-preset MusicManager enum is a MIXER state machine over ~13 stems, not 18 separate tracks. A WebAudio recreation should crossfade layers on the same loop clock, not swap files. This is a structural fact no ledger system holds.

### “⑤ crit **proc** rule (chance vs deterministic) — entirely absent.”

*4 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[cite-s27d/crit-proc-is-deterministic-on-a-full-meter-and-the-multiplier-is-web-only]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (android v0.682 uses 1.2 / 1.5 — do not port)**)
  - RR:195 blocker (5) files the crit PROC rule as 'chance vs deterministic — entirely absent'. It is deterministic, and there is no dice roll anywhere near it. Web §1t99s0§.as:3272, inside the damage roll §1t4475s0§ (:3255), is 'if(this.§1t4414s0§ == this.§1t4415s0§)' -> ':3274 return _loc2_ * 1.3 + this.§1t1243s0§;'. Those two members are reload and maxreload: the android twin getDamage (MCSPlayer.as:3198) has the same test in plain English at :3215 'if(this.reload == this.maxreload)'. Crit fires exactly when the Focus meter is FULL, every time, and §1t99s0§.as:3295-3297 zeroes the meter on the next swing, so a full bar buys exactly one crit. SEPARATE HAZARD, and it is a build-parity trap: the MULTIPLIER is web-only. Android MCSPlayer.as:3219 returns 'maxDamage * 1.2 + this.STAT_CRITICALDMG' for non-Light stances and :3221 'maxDamage * 1.5 + ...' for Light. Anyone reading crit out of the android blobs gets 1.2/1.5, not 1.3 — and the wiki's 1.3 happens to match the WEB build only.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3255, :3272-3274, :3295-3297 · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3198, :3215-3221`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters blocker (5) closes as CONTRACT: crit is deterministic on a full Focus meter, consumed by the swing that lands it. And CRIT_MULT = 1.3 must be labelled WEB v0.717 — an android-sourced port would ship 1.2/1.5.
- **`[[hunter-s27d/crit-is-deterministic-at-full-focus-not-a-proc]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - RR:195 skills-meters blocker (5) reads 'crit **proc** rule (chance vs deterministic) — entirely absent.' It is present and it is deterministic. §1t99s0§.as:3255-3277 is the whole damage roll: the ONLY randomness is the base roll at :3258 'var _loc1_:int = this.minDamage + Math.random() * this.damageRange;'. The crit branch at :3272 is 'if(this.§1t4414s0§ == this.§1t4415s0§)' - strict equality against maxreload, no dice - returning ':3274 return _loc2_ * 1.3 + this.§1t1243s0§;'. There is no probability term anywhere in the function. §1t4478s0§ at :3293-3297 then zeroes the meter on the same equality before applying the per-stance slash debit, so crits cannot chain: a crit costs the entire 192-point bar and the next one is 192 ticks away at the +1/tick fill rate (§1t99s0§.as:1203-1205), less whatever §1t4479s0§'s +6 refunds. The HUD says the same thing out loud - §1t113s0§.as:348-358 shows the black getString(175) = 'Critical Hit' caption and fires Snd_criticalDamage the moment the normalised bar reaches 1, i.e. the player is TOLD when the guaranteed crit is armed. §1t1243s0§ is the additive crit-damage stat (Android name STAT_CRITICALDMG, MCSPlayer.as:3219).
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3255-3277,:3293-3297,:1203-1205 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t113s0§.as:348-358 · docs/gauntlet/L0/index/strings.json["175"] · reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3219 · docs/research/REBUILD_READINESS.md:195`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters: delete blocker (5) from RR:195. CRIT_PROC is deterministic - crit iff focus == FOCUS_MAX - and the crit consumes the full bar. This is the correct place to record the finding that combat-core/crit-1.3 mis-aimed at RR:150-154.
- **`[[hunter-s27d/the-crit-proc-rule-is-deterministic-full-meter-not-a-chance-roll]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717, confirmed by the android twin**)
  - RR:195 skills-meters blocker ⑤ says the crit PROC rule is 'entirely absent' and asks whether it is chance or deterministic, and the crit finding in this packet declines to settle it, saying :3272 'tests an equality between two obfuscated members, not a probability'. The members are not unknown. The fifteen-parameter init at §1t99s0§.as:183-199 maps one-for-one onto android MCSPlayer.as:182-198, and §1t4478s0§/§1t4479s0§ are line-for-line deductReloadBar()/bonusReload() — android :3242-3244 'if(this.reload == this.maxreload) { this.reload = 0; }' is the same statement as web :3295-3297 with plain names. So §1t4414s0§ = reload and §1t4415s0§ = maxreload, and :3272 reads 'if the Focus meter is FULL'. Crit is DETERMINISTIC: a full meter guarantees x1.3 on the next hit, and the same full meter is zeroed by the spend that follows, so exactly one crit per fill. There is no probability anywhere in the path — Math.random() appears at :3281 for the damage RANGE roll and nowhere in the crit branch. The HUD agrees and is visible to the player: §1t113s0§.as:348-359 shows black 'Critical Hit' plus Snd_criticalDamage the moment fill >= 1, i.e. the game TELLS you the crit is armed, which only makes sense for a deterministic rule.

  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters blocker ⑤ CLOSES as CONTRACT, and it is the one this packet's crit finding explicitly left open. CRIT_PROC = deterministic on reload == maxreload, consumed by the same hit. Combined with the Focus finding this makes crit a resource the player spends, not a dice roll — which changes 
- **`[[skills-meters/crit-is-deterministic]]`** [A2] (6 critics)
  - Ledger blocker ⑤ is dead: crit is not a proc at all — it fires on the exact equality reload == maxreload and costs the whole meter, in the shipped web build.

### “② condition→DMG **multiplier** (current "evidence" confounds condition with a 2-level gap).”

*4 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/no-condition-damage-multiplier]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - There is no condition→damage multiplier anywhere in the code: getCondition() has exactly one reader in the whole tree and it is the tooltip; the only mechanical effect of wear is the binary broken state, and durability/slash-cost are three- and four-tier weapon-only data tables.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/Item.as:714-744 and :1481-1490 (grep -rn "getCondition()" . → 2 hits, both in Item.as) · data measured over reference/extracted/itemdata.xml`
  - Lane impact: L-items-equipment: blocker ② resolved in the negative — do not encode a multiplier; L-skills-meters gains the exact per-slash Focus cost table (72/96, floored at 48).
- **`[[itemdata/condition-has-no-damage-multiplier]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - There is no condition-to-damage multiplier anywhere in the engine: weapon damage is flat until the item breaks, at which point the weapon contributes nothing at all; the six condition tiers are display-only labels over remaining durability.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1863-1888 (getMinDamage / getDamageRange) + Item.as:1467-1496 (isBroken / getCondition) + Item.as:714-746 (getDesc)`

### “② elemental ATK-vs-DEF **formula**.”

*4 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[hunter/elemental-atk-vs-def-formula-held]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **both**)
  - The elemental ATK-vs-DEF formula is fully recovered, and nobody in this packet claimed it. Each element channel is mitigated independently by the matching elemental DEF, floored at 1 whenever the attacker carried any of that element (so an elemental hit always chips at least 1 per channel) and at 0 otherwise; the four channels are then summed with the physical damage into a single HP deduction. The defender's elemental DEF stats are themselves derived from the COUNTER element's ATK stat, which is the code-side form of the element wheel.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android_payload/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3347-3350, :3351-3381 and :3420 + reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StatsManager.as:1836`
  - Lane impact: rebirth-elements blocker ② closes OUTRIGHT — subtractive per-channel mitigation with a 1-point floor, plus the rule that your FIRE stat is what defends you against EARTH. That last line independently reproduces the artwork's clockwise wheel (fire counters earth, earth counters wind, wind counters wa
- **`[[progression/elemental-def-crosswheel]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - Elemental damage bypasses the DEF stat entirely and runs a parallel four-channel pipeline whose per-element defense is CROSS-WIRED to the opposite element's ATK stat — fireDef=waterAtk, windDef=earthAtk, waterDef=windAtk, earthDef=fireAtk — which is the elemental ATK-vs-DEF formula the rebirth-elements ledger lists as blocker ②.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3347-3357, :3415, :3420 and StatsManager.as:1836; web parity reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2270 with constants §1t1235s0§..§1t1242s0§ at :260-294`
  - Lane impact: L5 rebirth-elements blocker ② dies outright — the formula is held in BOTH builds with identical argument order, and it is a counter-wheel (Water beats Fire, Earth beats Wind, Wind beats Water, Fire beats Earth) rather than a curve. L1 combat-core: the damage pipeline is two-stage, physical DEF then 
- **`[[rebirth/element-counter-wheel]]`** [A2] (9 critics)
  - Your ATK in one element IS your defence against another — a rock-paper-scissors wheel that makes choosing a rebirth element simultaneously a choice of what you become immune to.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android_payload/scripts/StatsManager.as:1836, desktop confirm reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:2270; parameter order from MCSPlayer.as:260`
  - Lane impact: rebirth-elements blocker ② (the DEF half) and the Surface bullet's 'elements are TWO systems' note — the gear side and the rebirth side are the SAME stat ids (28-31 ATK, 200-203 DEF gear affixes), which is the link the ledger says was never made. Carried from PASS 1, re-verified in both bundles.
- **`[[rebirth/mitigation-flat-min1]]`** [A2] (9 critics)
  - Elemental mitigation is flat subtraction with a hard min-1 floor per channel, summed with physical damage into a single HP loss.

  - Lane impact: rebirth-elements blocker ② (the ATK-vs-DEF half) closed. Note the elemental floor is a hard 1, distinct from the physical getMinDamage() ladder — so the ledger's Ready bullet 'Undead/Red-Oni damage floor' is a different mechanism and should not be conflated. ### “⑤ Undead damage-reflect probability; arrow arc physics.”

*3 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[enemies-ai/undead-rule-is-not-reflect]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - The Undead blocker is wrong in kind, not merely unmeasured: grep 'reflect' over all 559 Android classes returns zero hits. The actual Undead-specific rule is a head-gate — a zombie takes HALF damage unless the hit lands on hat/head/neck — inverting the base class, which instead rewards a behead with ×1.5. JiangSi uses the standard ×1.5 and does not halve.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/EnemyZombie.as:493-518 (contrast NPC.as:451-453 and EnemyJiangSi.as:308-311)`
  - Lane impact: L0 enemies-ai: kills the undead half of blocker ⑤ by refuting its premise. There is no reflect mechanic and therefore no reflect probability to measure — planning a measurement pass for it would have been wasted work.
- **`[[enemies-ai/web-arrow-dual-gravity]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - In the shipped web v0.717 build knockdown arrows fly on HALF gravity (1.5) while normal arrows use 3 — the launch roll happens before the ballistic solve and feeds it — whereas the Android v0.682 decompile every cited number came from has a single unconditional GRAVITY.

This is also the first CONCRETE v0.682-vs-v0.717 behavioural difference found, which turns 'build parity is an unknown 
- **`[[questdata/archer-ai-firing-rule-and-gravity]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **android v0.682**)
  - The live archer AI firing rule and the global projectile gravity constant are both held, closing the ledger's 'arrow arc physics' item outright. Projectile.GRAVITY = 3 (px/frame^2), applied as velY += GRAVITY every update. EnemyArcher.fireArrow() fires from (mcs.x, mcs.y-60) at a target sampled at (target.mcs.x, target.mcs.y-60), solving a fixed-45-degree-ish ballistic: velA = sqrt(GRAVITY*|dx|/0.866), velXa = velA*0.866, t = |dx|/velXa, velYa = (dy - 0.5*GRAVITY*t*t)/t, sign-flipped when firing left, then a uniform aim jitter velYa += Math.random()*8 - 4. Above level 50 a coin flip sets Damage.LAUNCH_KNOCKDOWN on the arrow. EnemyGoblinThrower uses the identical solve; EnemyKanjiGuardian divides by 0.866/3 for a flatter arc.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/Projectile.as:8, ArrowProjectile.as:56, EnemyArcher.as:271, :277, :281, :286, EnemyGoblinThrower.as:555, EnemyKanjiGuardian.as:464`
  - Lane impact: L7 enemies-ai blocker ⑤ ('arrow arc physics') closes. L1 combat-core gains a hard gravity constant for every ballistic projectile. Directly refutes the stated honest-limit in questdata/projectile-launch-params.

### “④ **screen-shake** + **hitstop-on-landed-hit** (never catalogued);”

*3 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[art-anim-audio-feel/no-hitstop]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - There is no hitstop on a landed hit anywhere in the client: the only time-freeze is STATE_FROZEN, set at exactly two sites (both the battle-intro bar), plus the end-of-battle redFreeze; gotHit sets a 12-tick reaction counter and no global freeze.
  - Cite: `grep for hitstop|hitpause|freezeframe|slowmo|timeScale|hitFreeze over reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/*.as returns zero hits; STATE_FROZEN set only at StageManager.as:162 and :212; MCSPlayer.as:3719`
  - Lane impact: art-anim-audio-feel: the honest answer is 'the mechanism does not exist', so any hitstop in the recreation is an authored deviation and must be labelled. Graded PARTIAL, not HELD, because this is a negative from an exhaustive grep of one build rather than a positive artifact.
- **`[[art-anim-audio-feel/screen-shake-amplitude-is-inert]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - The screen-shake amplitude parameter is effectively dead for every in-game trigger: because shakeAmount = min(amt, counter) and every hard-coded call passes a duration of 24 or less against a default amt of 30, the amplitude is always just the remaining tick count — a pure linear ramp-down from the duration value.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StageManager.as:1200, :1225; call sites HeavenlySwordProjectile.as:76, MCSPlayer.as:911/:946/:4180, Trap_CrusherColumnAuto.as:90, Trap_CrusherBlockAuto.as:88, StatsManager.as:3535, Marker_RumbleTrigger.as:44`
  - Lane impact: art-anim-audio-feel: sharpens blocker ④. The recreation should implement shake as `offset = uniform(-t/2, +t/2)` on both axes where t is the REMAINING tick count — a 12-tick Cleave shake starts at ±5.5 px and decays to 0, it never reaches ±15. The 30 default only ever binds through Marker_RumbleTrig
- **`[[art-anim-audio-feel/screen-shake-catalogued]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - Screen-shake is fully catalogued: a uniform random offset within ±amt/2 on both axes every tick, default amplitude 30 px, auto-tapering over the final amt ticks via min(amt,counter), snapping back to the exact start position — and it keeps running while the action is frozen.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/StageManager.as:1200-1227 (startShake/doShake), :826-830 (stepped outside the STATE_FROZEN gate); triggers at MCSPlayer.as:911 (Cleave, 12), :946 (Earthquake jutsu, 24), :4180 (player launched, 12), Trap_CrusherColumnAuto.as:90 (6), Marker_RumbleTrigger.as:9`
  - Lane impact: art-anim-audio-feel blocker ④ half-deleted, and §1's UNKNOWN-UNKNOWNS bullet 'screen-shake and hitstop-on-landed-hit (never catalogued)' plus §5 Tier-1's 'whether there's screen-shake / hitstop' are both closed without any frame-measuring.

### “3. **The stance tables (dmg 0.9/1.2/1.4, reach 300/200/100) are MASTERY-9 CEILING values, not base.** A level-1 Shugyosha does Light ×0.5 / Balanced ×1.0 / Heavy ×1.4 (Heavy is fixed); Heavy's *reach* also scales ×0.5→×0”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[skills-meters/heavy-stance-damage-is-flat-and-only-heavy-reach-scales]]`** [A2] (4 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - RR:155 packs two different formulas into one sentence and gets the pairing backwards. In web v0.717 DAMAGE (§1t99s0§.as:3255-3271) light scales with light mastery (0.5 -> 0.9), balanced with balanced mastery (1.0 -> 1.2), and heavy is FLAT x1.4 with no mastery term at all - :3267-3270 fall through three cases to '_loc2_ = _loc1_ * 1.4;'. REACH (§1t99s0§.as:3091-3121) is the mirror image: light is FLAT x1.4 of base (:3094), balanced scales with balanced mastery (:3105), and heavy scales with heavy mastery (:3116) AND is clamped to a floor of 100 at :3117-3119. So exactly one of the three stances is mastery-flat in each table, and it is a different stance in each. The ledger's '(Heavy is fixed)' is true for damage only, and its 'Heavy's reach also scales x0.5 -> x0.9' is right but is the ONLY reach term it names.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3091-3121 and :3255-3271`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters + L5 combat-core. Model two separate 3-entry tables with a mastery term on a DIFFERENT stance in each, plus a hard reach floor of 100 on heavy. The ledger's reach triple 300/200/100 is not reproducible from these multipliers at any single base.
- **`[[t1-overturns-03-cite-s27d/mastery-runs-1-to-10-so-RR155s-level-1-floor-is-wrong-too]]`** [A2] (1 critic · build **web v0.717 (skilldata.xml row counts are android-sourced per C2; the formulas and the seed value are web)**)
  - Resolves the fork that s27b/stance-ceilings-are-mastery-10-not-mastery-9 correctly left open, and adds a second error in the same ledger sentence. (a) THE CAP IS 10: skilldata.xml skills 1000/1001/1002 (:4, :83, :162 — the three passive stance masteries fed to initPlayer as getStat(1000/1001/1002)) each have exactly ten <level> rows valued 1..10. So the web formulas 0.5+0.04m and 1+0.02m do reach 0.90 and 1.20, at m=10, and RR:155's 'MASTERY-9' label is an off-by-one rather than an unreachable ceiling. (b) THE FLOOR IS 1, NOT 0: the web build seeds all three masteries at value 1 (§1t1523s0§.as:1058-1060, mirrored at :2586-2588 for the second init path), so a fresh character is at m=1, not m=0. RR:155-156's 'A level-1 Shugyosha does Light x0.5 / Balanced x1.0 ... Heavy's reach also scales x0.5' is therefore wrong at both ends: the real floors are Light x0.54, Balanced x1.02 and heavy reach x0.54, and the real ceilings are x0.9 / x1.2 / x0.9 at m=10. Ship the two formulas with the domain m in [1,10]; do not ship the triple.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/skilldata.xml:4, :83, :162 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t1523s0§.as:1058-1060 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3262, :3265, :3116`
  - Lane impact: L2 skills-meters: RR:155-156 needs both numbers replaced, not just the '-9' label. STANCE_DMG_LIGHT(m) = 0.5 + 0.04m, STANCE_DMG_BALANCED(m) = 1 + 0.02m, STANCE_DMG_HEAVY = 1.4 flat, STANCE_REACH_HEAVY(m) = 0.5 + 0.04m, m in [1,10] seeded at 1. ### “⑤ sector↔room mapping rule (town=fixed sector vs wilderness=one-screen-per-sector).”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[s27b/a-territory-is-six-rooms-by-six-rooms-in-both-builds]]`** [A2] (5 critics · build **both — android v0.682 and web v0.717 agree**)
  - The same WarHexUI code that fixes the war board at 6x6 also fixes the sector-to-room mapping, which the ledger lists as an open world-exploration blocker. WarHexUI.as:195-196 derive the territory's zero-node as 'zeronodex = (this.mapid >> 12 & 0x0FFF) * 6' and 'zeronodey = (this.mapid & 0x0FFF) * 6', then :197 packs them back as 'zeronodex << 12 | zeronodey' and :208 walks the 6x6 loop with getShiftedNodeId(zeronodeid, i, j). The multiplier 6 is what makes each territory occupy a 6-by-6 block of node ids, so the war hex grid IS the room grid, one hex per room. The web build carries the identical transform at §1t7785s0§.as:196-197. This bears on RR:225 ⑤ 'sector<->room mapping rule', and it means the war board and the exploration room graph are the same coordinate space, not two systems to reconcile.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/WarHexUI.as:195-197, :208 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t7785s0§.as:196-197`
  - Lane impact: L-world-exploration: the sector<->room rule is 6x6 rooms per sector, with node id = (sectorX*6 + i) << 12 | (sectorY*6 + j). Same coordinate space as the war board.
- **`[[world-exploration/sector-room-mapping]]`** [A2] (6 critics)
  - Blocker ⑤ "sector↔room mapping rule" is dead, and the answer is neither option the ledger offered: a sector IS a node, a node owns an ordered list of rooms, and each room is one side-scrolling stage with a declared pixel width of 800-4000.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/android/112_Mobile_NodeData.bin:8-18 + reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/QuestEngine.as:1234-1240`
  - Lane impact: L1 world-exploration: closes blocker ⑤. Measured across the authored file: 44 nodes, 98 rooms, 94 with explicit <width>, min 800 max 4000 (modes 800×22, 1000×21, 1400×7, 1600×7) = 1 to 5 screens of scroll against the 800×480 native canvas. Rooms carry <bg> (30 distinct ids), optional <roomname> and

### “7. **`MAX_DAN_RANK=10` and `tenthGold=100` should be INFERRED, not CONTRACT.** The only wiki datum is garbled ("dan rank 5 (kyudan)" — kyudan=9th)”

  - RR:164-165 rules that 'MAX_DAN_RANK=10 and tenthGold=100 should be INFERRED, not CONTRACT', on the grounds that 'The only wiki datum is garbled ("dan rank 5 (kyudan)" — kyudan=9th)'. There is no need to rely on the wiki. The cap is in the WEB build's own rebirth routine: §1t1523s0§.as:2653 gates the increment on 'dan < 9', so dan runs 0..9, ten rungs, and android StatsManager.as:2209-2211 is the plain-named twin. The string table independently carries exactly ten consecutive titles, ids 455-464, (初段) through (十段), with 465 = 'Renshi' being unrelated, so the count is confirmed twice from two different artifact kinds. MAX_DAN_RANK = 10 is CONTRACT on target-build source. ### “**Sell price is NOT flat per item** — it scales with rarity/level (same sword 5c and 25c). Don't encode flat-per-type.”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/sell-price-formula]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - Sell price is (1 + affixModifierCount) x 10 for items of weight >= 10 and x 5 below that, with anything itemID < 3000 selling for a flat 1 — it does NOT scale with rarity or level, and it is identical in both builds.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/Item.as:1582-1593 (affix count at Item.as:307-310); web parity reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t17s0§.as:1977-1988`
  - Lane impact: L-economy (blocker ② half-closed) and L-items-equipment (blocker ③ closed). The ledger's own observation is reproduced exactly by the true mechanism: 5c = a light item with 0 modifiers (1+0)x5; 25c = the same item with 4 modifiers (1+4)x5. The mechanism is affix count x weight class, not rarity/leve
- **`[[items-equipment/sell-price-corrects-section3-item9]]`** [A2] (12 critics)
  - The same formula corrects §3 correction #9: sell price scales with MODIFIER COUNT and a single weight>=10 step — it does NOT scale with level at all (5c = 0 mods weight<10; 25c = 4 mods weight<10).
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/Item.as:1582-1593`
  - Lane impact: L4 (economy) — a builder encoding a level term implements a different curve; the observed 5c/25c pair is fully explained by modifier count alone.

### “**Multi-target damage rule** (one arc hits N enemies — full damage each? falloff toward the tip?).”

- **`[[combat-core/multi-target-full-damage]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - Blocker ④ is dead: one slash tests the whole enemyList and neutralList, each enemy can be hit at most once, and every victim takes the SAME Damage object — full damage each, no falloff toward the tip.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPCManager.as:1204-1295 (damage rolled once at MCSPlayer.as:2699); combo cap StatsManager.as:4298-4306`
  - Lane impact: L5 — also yields the cheap pre-filter |Δx| < 200 world units, and grounds the 'combo rules' Ready bullet: combo = count of DISTINCT enemies hit per stroke, accumulated into a persistent counter capped at 999.

### “**Hit-registration window** (instant at release vs an active sweep an enemy can dodge into/out of).”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[actionscript/hit-registration-window]]`** [A2] (2 critics)
  - Hit registration is instantaneous at slash resolution, evaluated by walking the released polyline point-by-point — there is no swept active window an enemy can dodge into or out of — and resolution walks the paperdoll part list FRONT-to-BACK so the topmost part struck decides the hit (which is the actual mechanism behind 'shielder legs / undead heads' weak points).
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPC.as:301-321`
  - Lane impact: L-combat-core, L-enemies-ai, L-items-equipment (weak points are literally paperdoll slots, so the weak-point table and the equipment layer table are the same table).
- **`[[combat-core/hit-registration-instant-at-endslash]]`** [A2] (8 critics)
  - The hit-registration window is answered: there is no active sweep and no per-frame hitbox — the entire polyline resolves in one instant inside endSlash(), against wherever enemies stand at that moment.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:2766-2814 (the only call sites of NPCManager.checkHit / checkPlayerHit)`
  - Lane impact: L5 — an enemy cannot dodge into or out of a stroke mid-sweep; deductReloadBar() is charged in the same call, so the meter cost lands at resolution, not at input.

  - The open question behind the packet's hp-less-combat-rows claim is answerable and I answered it. Spawn level for combat NPCs comes from the QUEST WAVE, not from npcdata. NPCManager.as:110-131 reads wave[i].enemy[j].lvl into new NPCHolder(id,fatal,xpos,lvl); :407/411/433/437 pass npcHolder.getLvl() into createEnemy/createObjective, which call actor.setLevel(lvl) when lvl != 0 (:877-879, :931-934). 160 of the 169 <enemy> blocks in questdata.xml carry <lvl>. Concretely: Gojo Takamori npc 190 spawns at lvl 25 (questdata.xml:26101 id, :26105 lvl) and npc 191 at 35 (:25862 / :25866); Magoichi npc 188 at 35, npc 810 at 27. Friendlies never need one — FriendlyElite.as:70 and FriendlyArcher.as:58 call setLevel(StatsManager.getLevel()), so allies scale to the player, and 7 of the 12 skill-carrying hp-less rows are ai 30 / ai 34 = FriendlyElite / FriendlyArcher (NPCManager.as:608-621). NPC.setLevel at :239-259 then rescales damage, defense and maxhp by lvl/level and hard-clamps all three to 1 when lvl <= 10.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/NPCManager.as:110-131, :407, :877-879, :931-934 and NPC.as:239-259, FriendlyElite.as:70, FriendlyArcher.as:58; reference/extracted/questdata.xml:26101 and :26105 (npc 190 at <lvl>25</lvl>), :25862 and :25866 (npc 191 at <lvl>35</lvl>)`
  - Lane impact: L8 enemies-ai — the level→stat transfer function is NOT absent: it is NPC.setLevel's factor = lvl/level (x0.9 below level 21), applied to damage, defense and maxhp, plus EnemyElite.init's boss/level bands (x1.4 above 70, x1.3 above 50, x1.2 above 30, x0.8 below 10). Combined with the quest-wave <lvl

- **`[[npcdata/five-stances-reversed-and-dual]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **android v0.682**)
  - Skills 1500 and 1501 are not merely unnamed enemy-only entries — they are two additional STANCES. EnemyElite.init dispatches getMastery(1500) > 0 to setStance(REVERSED_STANCE) and getMastery(1501) > 0 to setStance(DUAL_STANCE), with the same dispatch in EnemyPlayer and FriendlyElite, and the build ships dedicated animation payloads for both. The shipped game therefore has FIVE stances (light, balanced, heavy, reversed, dual), not three. Carriers in npcdata: 1500 -> Aboshi (:3662) and Magoichi (:3898); 1501 -> Gojo Takamori (:3934 and :3956).

### “**Damage math:** Physical = (ATK + WeaponDMG) × stanceMult; Heavy defender Defense ×0.8 (one way);”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-A/web-stance-triangle-has-a-second-0p9-arm]]`** [A2] (3 critics · build **web v0.717 (contrasted with android v0.682)**)
  - The stance counter-triangle has TWO arms in web v0.717, not one. Winning matchups (Light vs Balanced, Balanced vs Heavy, Heavy vs Light) take damage x1.2; LOSING matchups (Light vs Heavy, Balanced vs Light, Heavy vs Balanced) take damage x0.9. Android v0.682 has only the x1.2 arm - grep for '*= 0.9' across MCSPlayer.as returns zero hits. Both arms are applied before the defense subtraction and before the min-damage floor. REBUILD_READINESS.md:74 carries neither term; a Sim written from that bullet is wrong by 20% on countered matchups and by 10% on countering ones.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3788-3798 and :4379-4389 vs reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3701-3707`
  - Lane impact: L1/L5 combat-core: RR:74 needs both triangle arms added, and the pair must be applied pre-defense, pre-floor.
- **`[[NF-B/stance-triangle-is-defender-keyed-and-web-adds-a-losing-side]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **both**)
  - The stance counter-triangle runs in the DEFENDER's frame, and the web build has a losing side android lacks. Both x1.2 branches sit inside gotHit (android MCSPlayer.as:3580; web §1t99s0§ — _loc11_ is assigned param2.stance at :3754, param2 being the Damage object), so `this.stance` is the defender and the attacker's stance is the operand. The shipped rule is: Balanced beats Light, Heavy beats Balanced, Light beats Heavy — confirmed independently by the game's own copy, strings.json["6001"]/["6003"]/["6005"]. The WEB build additionally applies x0.9 on each reversed matchup (§1t99s0§.as:3796-3798), which android has no counterpart for: six ordered outcomes, not three. Both terms land BEFORE the DEF subtraction and the min-damage floor.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:3754, :3788-3794, :3796-3798, :3800-3804 vs reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/MCSPlayer.as:3580, :3654, :3701-3709 + docs/gauntlet/L0/index/strings.json["6001"], ["6003"], ["6005"]`
  - Lane impact: L1 combat-core: the :74 KNOWN-KNOWN damage line is missing a rock-paper-scissors term entirely, and the web build's version is a 6-way table (x1.2 / x0.9 / x1.0), not the 3-way the android decompile shows.

### “- **Blockers:** ① **ALL AUDIO** — every SFX + music param is INFERRED, no composer sourced.”

*2 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

- **`[[NF-F/audio-recovered-13-waveforms-against-43-web-cue-names]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **both (waveforms android blob; cue names web source)**)
  - The SFX side of the audio blocker is closed on both halves, with a precise residual. The DECRYPT is not a whole-file XOR — ResourceManager.decryptByteArray touches only bytes 3..255, swapping ba[i] with ba[i+256] when i%11==0 and then XORing with charCodeAt(1) of the resource name, which is why the raw blob already reads 'CWS' and a naive XOR fails. For Mobile_SoundResource the key is 'o' = 111; the result is a valid CWS v17 SWF (filelen 29,631, inflate 29,623) carrying 13 DefineSound tags and a SymbolClass table naming every one: punch, fart, hitmetal, goodkoto, slash1, slash2, die1, refund, die3, slash3, sumo, die2, deflect. Separately, the WEB payload's own source names 43 distinct "Snd_*" cues at live call sites. So the residual is exact: 13 waveforms recovered, 43 cue names known, 30 cues with no PCM in blob 122.
  - Cite: `reference/extracted/android/122_Mobile_SoundResource.bin decoded per reference/decompiled/shs3_android/scripts/ResourceManager.as:200-217 and Mobile.as:352 + my grep over reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/ with call sites at reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t113s0§.as:472 and :478`
  - Lane impact: L6 art-anim-audio-feel ①: 'every SFX is INFERRED' is false twice over — 13 SFX exist as real PCM and 43 are named with trigger points in web source. The honest remaining gap is 30 named cues without waveforms, not 'all audio'.
- **`[[NF-G/43-web-sfx-cues-refiled-without-the-dead-s0-5-clause]]`** [A2] (2 critics · build **web v0.717**)
  - Re-filing the SFX cue inventory stripped of the superseded audio-encryption claim that auto-refuted its parent. The web v0.717 payload's decompiled classes carry exactly 43 distinct "Snd_*" string literals, each at a live call site, so the cue set and its trigger map are HELD from primary web source: Snd_addStat, Snd_back, Snd_birds1/2/3, Snd_bowFire, Snd_bowFireMany, Snd_challenge, Snd_click, Snd_criticalDamage, Snd_deflect, Snd_die1/2/3, Snd_drop, Snd_equip, Snd_error, Snd_fart, Snd_goodKoto, Snd_hitMetal, Snd_jump, Snd_mail, Snd_menu, Snd_menuClose, Snd_mining, Snd_ninja, Snd_notification, Snd_punch, Snd_put, Snd_refund, Snd_rimshot, Snd_sadTrombone, Snd_slash1/2/3, Snd_smallClick, Snd_smokebomb, Snd_sumo, Snd_upgradeSkill, Snd_wrAttack, Snd_wrDeploy, Snd_wrMove, Snd_wrScout. Trigger example verified: §1t113s0§.as:472 fires Snd_smallClick on a legal stance switch and :478 fires Snd_error on a refused one; §1t113s0§.as:352 fires Snd_criticalDamage the tick the Focus bar reaches full. Per correction C3 the waveforms are recovered separately, so this is a manifest, not a consolation prize.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/ (own grep -rhoE '"Snd_[A-Za-z0-9_]+"' | sort -u | wc -l -> 43) + reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t113s0§.as:472, :478, :352`
  - Lane impact: L6 art-anim-audio-feel: 'every SFX is INFERRED' is false. A 43-cue manifest with real trigger points exists in the target build and can be authored against directly.

### “(3) Focus numerics (max/fill/switch-cost).”

*1 surviving finding(s) hit this line.*

It fills +1 per sim tick while below max (:1203-1205, 'if(this.§1t4414s0§ < this.§1t4415s0§){++this.§1t4414s0§;'), so 0 -> full is 192 ticks. The stance switch costs exactly 48 (:3162 'this.§1t4414s0§ -= 48;'), which is why the gate at :3050 and :3060 tests >= 48 - one quarter of the bar per switch, four switches from full. A slash at full meter zeroes it outright (:3295-3297); otherwise it debits by stance - Light slashUsage/2, Balanced/Dual slashUsage, Heavy/Reversed slashUsage*1.5 (:3299-3311), clamped at 0. §1t4479s0§ (:3318-3325) adds a flat +6, clamped to max. The HUD normalizes as (v-48)/(max-48), i.e. the DISPLAYED bar is empty at v<=48 and full at 192 (§1t113s0§.as:328). Nothing here is INFERRED.
  - Cite: `reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t99s0§.as:249-250,:1203-1205,:3162,:3295-3316,:3318-3325 · reference/decompiled/shs3_payload/scripts/§1t113s0§.as:328`
